Bible Quiz Questions – New Testament – Volume 2: Acts and Pauline Letters
Acts–Philemon · 115 chapters · 920 questions
Collection index and Volume 1
Each chapter has eight questions: three Easy, three Medium and two Hard. Question order and multiple-choice answer positions are mixed. Hard questions use closer recall, comparison of passages or careful interpretation.
Quotation questions marked WEB follow the World English Bible. Interpretation questions paraphrase the passage; accept a clearly equivalent answer when exact wording is not being tested.
Read each passage in its literary and historical setting. Theological explanations draw on Scripture and the Gospel Worldview course summary. Course-specific views and disputed readings are identified rather than presented as universal agreement. A character’s words are not automatically the narrator’s teaching.
Sources: Gospel Worldview course summary · World English Bible · Old Testament collection
Examples of Bible-edition notes for disputed passages: Mark 16 · John 7:53–8:11 · 1 John 5:6–8
Acts
Acts 1
Q1. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “While they were looking ______ into the sky as he went, behold, ______ men stood by them in white clothing,” (1:10, WEB) Medium
Answer: steadfastly; two. The wording is “While they were looking steadfastly into the sky as he went, behold, two men stood by them in white clothing,” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q2. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… midst of the disciples (and the number of names was about one first twenty), and said,” (1:15, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “… midst of the disciples (and the number of names was about one hundred twenty), and said,” It says hundred, not first.
Q3. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… into the upper room, where they were staying; that is Peter, John, ______, Andrew, Philip, Thomas, Bartholomew, Matthew, James the son of Alphaeus, Simon the Zealot, …”? (1:13, WEB) Medium
A) James B) Hannah C) Zerubbabel D) Martha
Answer: A. James. The passage specifies James: “… into the upper room, where they were staying; that is Peter, John, James, Andrew, Philip, Thomas, Bartholomew, Matthew, James the son of Alphaeus, Simon the Zealot, …”
Q4. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… which he was received up, after he had given commandment through the ______ Spirit to the apostles whom he had chosen.” (1:2, WEB) Easy
Answer: Holy. The verse reads, “… which he was received up, after he had given commandment through the Holy Spirit to the apostles whom he had chosen.”
Q5. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… Spirit has come upon you. You will be witnesses to me in ______, in all Judea and Samaria, and to the uttermost parts of the earth."”? (1:8, WEB) Easy
A) Cana B) Lystra C) Jerusalem D) Colossae
Answer: C. Jerusalem. Acts 1:8 uses Jerusalem: “… Spirit has come upon you. You will be witnesses to me in Jerusalem, in all Judea and Samaria, and to the uttermost parts of the earth."”
Q6. Multiple choice: What task does Jesus give when the disciples ask about restoring the kingdom's timing? (1:6-8) Hard
A) Establish witness without any need of the Spirit. B) Receive the Spirit's power and become witnesses from Jerusalem to the ends of the earth. C) Calculate the unknown time before bearing witness. D) Remain permanently within one ethnic group.
Answer: B. Receive the Spirit's power and become witnesses from Jerusalem to the ends of the earth. Jesus redirects attention from control of the timetable to Spirit-empowered witness. The mission expands geographically and across peoples.
Q7. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “They put forward two, Joseph called Barsabbas, who was surnamed Justus, and ______.” (1:23); “… the lot fell on Matthias, and he was numbered with the eleven ______.” (1:26, WEB) Hard
Answer: Matthias; apostles. The first passage reads “They put forward two, Joseph called Barsabbas, who was surnamed Justus, and Matthias.”; the second reads “… the lot fell on Matthias, and he was numbered with the eleven apostles.”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Q8. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “For John indeed baptized in ______, but you will be baptized in the Holy Spirit not many days from now."” (1:5, WEB) Easy
Answer: water. The cited words are “For John indeed baptized in water, but you will be baptized in the Holy Spirit not many days from now."”
Acts 2
Q1. Multiple choice: How does Peter connect Pentecost with Joel's promise? (2:14-21, 32-39) Hard
A) The crowd is told that Jesus remained dead. B) The Spirit is presented as the apostles' private possession. C) The outpouring of the Spirit is explained through the promise and the exaltation of Jesus. D) Peter says Joel is unrelated to the event.
Answer: C. The outpouring of the Spirit is explained through the promise and the exaltation of Jesus. Peter joins Scripture, Jesus' resurrection and the Spirit's gift. Christians differ on some baptismal and charismatic questions; the sermon's call to repentance and the gift of the Spirit are explicit.
Q2. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “"Men of ______, hear these words! Jesus of Nazareth, a man approved by God to you by mighty works and wonders and signs which God did …”? (2:22, WEB) Medium
A) Eutychus B) Martha C) Israel D) Levi
Answer: C. Israel. The passage specifies Israel: “"Men of Israel, hear these words! Jesus of Nazareth, a man approved by God to you by mighty works and wonders and signs which God did …”
Q3. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… will not leave my soul in Hades, Neither will you allow your gracious One to see decay.” (2:27, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “… will not leave my soul in Hades, Neither will you allow your Holy One to see decay.” It says Holy, not gracious.
Q4. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “… the last days, says God, That I will pour out my ______ on all ______. Your sons and your daughters will prophesy. Your young men will …” (2:17, WEB) Medium
Answer: Spirit; flesh. The wording is “… the last days, says God, That I will pour out my Spirit on all flesh. Your sons and your daughters will prophesy. Your young men will …” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q5. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “Parthians, Medes, Elamites, and people from Mesopotamia, Judea, Cappadocia, Pontus, ______,” (2:9, WEB) Easy
Answer: Asia. The cited words are “Parthians, Medes, Elamites, and people from Mesopotamia, Judea, Cappadocia, Pontus, Asia,”
Q6. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “Tongues like ______ appeared and were distributed to them, and one sat on each of them.” (2:3, WEB) Easy
Answer: fire. The verse reads, “Tongues like fire appeared and were distributed to them, and one sat on each of them.”
Q7. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “he foreseeing this spoke about the ______ of the Christ, that neither was his soul left in Hades, nor did his flesh see decay.” (2:31); “… know assuredly that God has made him both Lord and Christ, this ______ whom you crucified."” (2:36, WEB) Hard
A) resurrection; Judas B) resurrection; Jesus C) judgment; Jesus D) Jesus; resurrection
Answer: B. resurrection; Jesus. Verse 31 reads “he foreseeing this spoke about the resurrection of the Christ, that neither was his soul left in Hades, nor did his flesh see decay.”; verse 36 reads “… know assuredly that God has made him both Lord and Christ, this Jesus whom you crucified."”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q8. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… to them, "You men of Judea, and all you who dwell at ______, let this be known to you, and listen to my words.”? (2:14, WEB) Easy
A) Caesarea B) Sinai C) Tarshish D) Jerusalem
Answer: D. Jerusalem. Acts 2:14 uses Jerusalem: “… to them, "You men of Judea, and all you who dwell at Jerusalem, let this be known to you, and listen to my words.”
Acts 3
Q1. Multiple choice: Which word completes “Leaping up, he ______, and began to walk. He entered with them into the temple, walking, leaping, and praising God.”? (3:8, WEB) Easy
A) Jesus B) Righteous C) denied D) stood
Answer: D. stood. Acts 3:8 uses stood: “Leaping up, he stood, and began to walk. He entered with them into the temple, walking, leaping, and praising God.”
Q2. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… to ask gifts for the needy of those who entered into the ______.” (3:2, WEB) Easy
Answer: temple. The verse reads, “… to ask gifts for the needy of those who entered into the temple.”
Q3. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “… gifts for the ______ at the Beautiful Gate of the temple. They were filled with wonder and amazement at what had ______ to him.” (3:10, WEB) Medium
Answer: needy; happened. The wording is “… gifts for the needy at the Beautiful Gate of the temple. They were filled with wonder and amazement at what had happened to him.” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q4. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “By faith in his name has his name made this man strong, whom you see and know. Yes, the faith which is through him has given …” (3:16, WEB) Medium
Answer: True. The actual wording is “By faith in his name has his name made this man strong, whom you see and know. Yes, the faith which is through him has given …”
Q5. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “For ______ indeed said to the fathers, 'The Lord God will raise up a prophet for you from among your brothers, like me. You shall listen …” (3:22); “God, having raised up his ______, Jesus, sent him to you first, to bless you, in turning away everyone of you from your wickedness."” (3:26, WEB) Hard
Answer: Moses; servant. The first passage reads “For Moses indeed said to the fathers, 'The Lord God will raise up a prophet for you from among your brothers, like me. You shall listen …”; the second reads “God, having raised up his servant, Jesus, sent him to you first, to bless you, in turning away everyone of you from your wickedness."”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Q6. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… Servant Jesus, whom you delivered up, and denied in the presence of ______, when he had determined to release him.”? (3:13, WEB) Medium
A) Ithamar B) Lazarus C) Pilate D) Malachi
Answer: C. Pilate. The passage specifies Pilate: “… Servant Jesus, whom you delivered up, and denied in the presence of Pilate, when he had determined to release him.”
Q7. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “But ______ said, "Silver and gold have I none, but what I have, that I give you. In the name of Jesus Christ of Nazareth, get …” (3:6, WEB) Easy
Answer: Peter. The cited words are “But Peter said, "Silver and gold have I none, but what I have, that I give you. In the name of Jesus Christ of Nazareth, get …”
Q8. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “… things which God announced by the mouth of all his prophets, that ______ should suffer, he thus fulfilled.” (3:18); “… all things, which God spoke long ago by the mouth of his ______ prophets.” (3:21, WEB) Hard
A) Christ; holy B) Christ; faithful C) Barnabas; holy D) holy; Christ
Answer: A. Christ; holy. Verse 18 reads “… things which God announced by the mouth of all his prophets, that Christ should suffer, he thus fulfilled.”; verse 21 reads “… all things, which God spoke long ago by the mouth of his holy prophets.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Acts 4
Q1. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “who by the mouth of your ______, David, said, 'Why do the nations rage, And the peoples plot a vain thing?” (4:25); “For truly, in this city against your ______ servant, Jesus, whom you anointed, both Herod and Pontius Pilate, with the Gentiles and the people of Israel, …” (4:27, WEB) Hard
A) servant; humble B) holy; servant C) daughters; holy D) servant; holy
Answer: D. servant; holy. Verse 25 reads “who by the mouth of your servant, David, said, 'Why do the nations rage, And the peoples plot a vain thing?”; verse 27 reads “For truly, in this city against your holy servant, Jesus, whom you anointed, both Herod and Pontius Pilate, with the Gentiles and the people of Israel, …”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q2. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… let them go, finding no way to punish them, because of the people; for everyone glorified God for that which was done.” (4:21, WEB) Medium
Answer: True. The actual wording is “… let them go, finding no way to punish them, because of the people; for everyone glorified God for that which was done.”
Q3. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “Now when they saw the boldness of ______ and ______, and had perceived that they were unlearned and ignorant men, they marveled. They recognized that they …” (4:13, WEB) Medium
Answer: Peter; John. The wording is “Now when they saw the boldness of Peter and John, and had perceived that they were unlearned and ignorant men, they marveled. They recognized that they …” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q4. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “They laid hands on them, and put them in ______ until the next day, for it was now evening.” (4:3, WEB) Easy
Answer: custody. The verse reads, “They laid hands on them, and put them in custody until the next day, for it was now evening.”
Q5. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… them, that from now on they don't speak to anyone in this ______."”? (4:17, WEB) Medium
A) name B) judge C) filled D) today
Answer: A. name. The passage specifies name: “… them, that from now on they don't speak to anyone in this name."”
Q6. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “The multitude of those who believed were of one ______ and soul. Not one of them claimed that anything of the things which he possessed was …” (4:32); “Joses, who by the ______ was surnamed Barnabas (which is, being interpreted, Son of Encouragement), a Levite, a man of Cyprus by race,” (4:36, WEB) Hard
Answer: heart; apostles. The first passage reads “The multitude of those who believed were of one heart and soul. Not one of them claimed that anything of the things which he possessed was …”; the second reads “Joses, who by the apostles was surnamed Barnabas (which is, being interpreted, Son of Encouragement), a Levite, a man of Cyprus by race,”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Q7. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… the people of Israel, that in the name of Jesus Christ of ______, whom you crucified, whom God raised from the dead, in him does this …”? (4:10, WEB) Easy
A) Iconium B) Cana C) Joppa D) Nazareth
Answer: D. Nazareth. Acts 4:10 uses Nazareth: “… the people of Israel, that in the name of Jesus Christ of Nazareth, whom you crucified, whom God raised from the dead, in him does this …”
Q8. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “Annas the high ______ was there, with Caiaphas, John, Alexander, and as many as were relatives of the high priest.” (4:6, WEB) Easy
Answer: priest. The cited words are “Annas the high priest was there, with Caiaphas, John, Alexander, and as many as were relatives of the high priest.”
Acts 5
Q1. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “Multitudes also came together from the cities around ______, bringing ______ people, and those who were tormented by unclean spirits: and they were all healed.” (5:16, WEB) Medium
Answer: Jerusalem; sick. The wording is “Multitudes also came together from the cities around Jerusalem, bringing sick people, and those who were tormented by unclean spirits: and they were all healed.” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q2. Multiple choice: What makes the deaths of Ananias and Sapphira a warning about the community's holiness? (5:1-11) Hard
A) Peter tells leaders to execute dishonest donors. B) The narrator treats deceit as harmless. C) Every believer was required to surrender every last possession. D) They deceive the community in a way Peter identifies as lying to the Holy Spirit.
Answer: D. They deceive the community in a way Peter identifies as lying to the Holy Spirit. Peter says the property and proceeds had been under their control; the issue is deceit, not failure to surrender every possession. The episode does not authorize church leaders to punish people violently.
Q3. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “But Peter said, "______, why has Satan filled your heart to lie to the Holy Spirit, and to keep back part of the price of the …” (5:3, WEB) Easy
Answer: Ananias. The verse reads, “But Peter said, "Ananias, why has Satan filled your heart to lie to the Holy Spirit, and to keep back part of the price of the …”
Q4. Multiple choice: Which word completes “By the hands of the ______ many signs and wonders were done among the people. They were all with one accord in Solomon's porch.”? (5:12, WEB) Easy
A) apostles B) Solomon's C) words D) witnesses
Answer: A. apostles. Acts 5:12 uses apostles: “By the hands of the apostles many signs and wonders were done among the people. They were all with one accord in Solomon's porch.”
Q5. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… called the council together, and all the senate of the children of ______, and sent to the prison to have them brought.”? (5:21, WEB) Medium
A) Zephaniah B) Adam C) Israel D) Agrippa
Answer: C. Israel. The passage specifies Israel: “… called the council together, and all the senate of the children of Israel, and sent to the prison to have them brought.”
Q6. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “About ______ hours later, his wife, not knowing what had happened, came in.” (5:7, WEB) Easy
Answer: three. The cited words are “About three hours later, his wife, not knowing what had happened, came in.”
Q7. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “… have filled Jerusalem with your teaching, and intend to bring this man's ______ on us."” (5:28); “… his right hand to be a Prince and a Savior, to give ______ to Israel, and remission of sins.” (5:31, WEB) Hard
A) blood; perished B) blood; repentance C) repentance; blood D) hair; repentance
Answer: B. blood; repentance. Verse 28 reads “… have filled Jerusalem with your teaching, and intend to bring this man's blood on us."”; verse 31 reads “… his right hand to be a Prince and a Savior, to give repentance to Israel, and remission of sins.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q8. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… when the high priest, the captain of the temple, and the chief judge heard these words, they were very perplexed about them and what might become …” (5:24, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “… when the high priest, the captain of the temple, and the chief priests heard these words, they were very perplexed about them and what might become …” It says priests, not judge.
Acts 6
Q1. Multiple choice: How does the response to neglected widows join practical justice with the church's mission? (6:1-7) Hard
A) The widows are blamed for needing food. B) The apostles permanently abandon the teaching of the word. C) Practical care is declared unrelated to faithfulness. D) The community appoints trusted people to address the need while the apostles attend to prayer and the word.
Answer: D. The community appoints trusted people to address the need while the apostles attend to prayer and the word. The complaint is taken seriously instead of being dismissed as unspiritual. The course understands communal care as part of the church's life, not a distraction from all spiritual concerns.
Q2. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “The twelve summoned the multitude of the ______ and said, "It is not appropriate for us to forsake the word of God and serve tables.” (6:2, WEB) Easy
Answer: disciples. The verse reads, “The twelve summoned the multitude of the disciples and said, "It is not appropriate for us to forsake the word of God and serve tables.”
Q3. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “______, full of faith and power, performed great wonders and signs among the people.”? (6:8, WEB) Medium
A) Nicodemus B) Aquila C) Stephen D) Abel
Answer: C. Stephen. The passage specifies Stephen: “Stephen, full of faith and power, performed great wonders and signs among the people.”
Q4. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “… induced men to say, "We have heard him speak blasphemous words against ______ and God."” (6:11); “… man never stops speaking blasphemous words against this holy place and the ______.” (6:13, WEB) Hard
A) Moses; instruction B) Elijah; law C) law; Moses D) Moses; law
Answer: D. Moses; law. Verse 11 reads “… induced men to say, "We have heard him speak blasphemous words against Moses and God."”; verse 13 reads “… man never stops speaking blasphemous words against this holy place and the law.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q5. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… Holy Spirit, Philip, Prochorus, Nicanor, Timon, Parmenas, and Nicolaus, a proselyte of ______;”? (6:5, WEB) Easy
A) Nineveh B) Smyrna C) Asia D) Antioch
Answer: D. Antioch. Acts 6:5 uses Antioch: “… Holy Spirit, Philip, Prochorus, Nicanor, Timon, Parmenas, and Nicolaus, a proselyte of Antioch;”
Q6. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “… disciples multiplied in Jerusalem exceedingly. A great company of the ______ were obedient to the ______.” (6:7, WEB) Medium
Answer: priests; faith. The wording is “… disciples multiplied in Jerusalem exceedingly. A great company of the priests were obedient to the faith.” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q7. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… of the Cyrenians, of the Alexandrians, and of those of Cilicia and Berea arose, disputing with Stephen.” (6:9, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “… of the Cyrenians, of the Alexandrians, and of those of Cilicia and Asia arose, disputing with Stephen.” It says Asia, not Berea.
Q8. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… from among you, brothers, seven men of good report, full of the ______ Spirit and of wisdom, whom we may appoint over this business.” (6:3, WEB) Easy
Answer: Holy. The cited words are “… from among you, brothers, seven men of good report, full of the Holy Spirit and of wisdom, whom we may appoint over this business.”
Acts 7
Q1. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “______ was instructed in all the ______ of the Egyptians. He was mighty in his words and works.” (7:22, WEB) Medium
Answer: Moses; wisdom. The wording is “Moses was instructed in all the wisdom of the Egyptians. He was mighty in his words and works.” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q2. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… of all his afflictions, and gave him favor and wisdom before Pharaoh, ______ of Egypt. He made him governor over Egypt and all his house.” (7:10, WEB) Easy
Answer: king. The cited words are “… of all his afflictions, and gave him favor and wisdom before Pharaoh, king of Egypt. He made him governor over Egypt and all his house.”
Q3. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “Moses fled at this saying, and became a ______ in the land of Midian, where he became the father of two sons.”? (7:29, WEB) Medium
A) oracles B) stranger C) three D) idol
Answer: B. stranger. The passage specifies stranger: “Moses fled at this saying, and became a stranger in the land of Midian, where he became the father of two sons.”
Q4. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “… as for this Moses, who led us out of the land of ______, we don't know what has become of him.'” (7:40); “… brought in with Joshua when they entered into the possession of the ______, whom God drove out before the face of our fathers, to the days …” (7:45, WEB) Hard
A) Egypt; Jesus B) Egypt; nations C) nations; Egypt D) Philippi; nations
Answer: B. Egypt; nations. Verse 40 reads “… as for this Moses, who led us out of the land of Egypt, we don't know what has become of him.'”; verse 45 reads “… brought in with Joshua when they entered into the possession of the nations, whom God drove out before the face of our fathers, to the days …”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q5. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “"This Moses, whom they died, saying, 'Who made you a ruler and a judge?'--God has sent him as both a ruler and a deliverer by the …” (7:35, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “"This Moses, whom they refused, saying, 'Who made you a ruler and a judge?'--God has sent him as both a ruler and a deliverer by the …” It says refused, not died.
Q6. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… From there, when his father was dead, God moved him into this ______, where you are now living.” (7:4, WEB) Easy
Answer: land. The verse reads, “… From there, when his father was dead, God moved him into this land, where you are now living.”
Q7. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… and laid in the tomb that Abraham bought for a price in ______ from the children of Hamor of Shechem.”? (7:16, WEB) Easy
A) copper B) silver C) iron D) stones
Answer: B. silver. Acts 7:16 uses silver: “… and laid in the tomb that Abraham bought for a price in silver from the children of Hamor of Shechem.”
Q8. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “… your fathers persecute? They killed those who foretold the coming of the ______ One, of whom you have now become betrayers and murderers.” (7:52); “… witnesses placed their garments at the feet of a young man named ______.” (7:58, WEB) Hard
Answer: Righteous; Saul. The first passage reads “… your fathers persecute? They killed those who foretold the coming of the Righteous One, of whom you have now become betrayers and murderers.”; the second reads “… witnesses placed their garments at the feet of a young man named Saul.”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Acts 8
Verse 37 appears in some manuscript traditions and Bible editions but is absent from many early witnesses. The question set does not require that disputed wording.
Q1. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “For unclean ______ came out of many of those who had them. They came out, crying with a loud voice. Many who had been paralyzed and …” (8:7, WEB) Easy
Answer: spirits. The cited words are “For unclean spirits came out of many of those who had them. They came out, crying with a loud voice. Many who had been paralyzed and …”
Q2. Multiple choice: What distinction is needed when comparing the Samaritans' reception of the Spirit with other Acts accounts? (8:14-17) Hard
A) Every reception of the Spirit in Acts follows identical details. B) The Samaritans are said never to receive the Spirit. C) The narrative records this event's sequence without explicitly making every detail a universal timetable. D) The story means Samaritans cannot belong to the church.
Answer: C. The narrative records this event's sequence without explicitly making every detail a universal timetable. Acts depicts important boundary-crossing moments with differing sequences. Christian traditions debate their implications; a description should not silently become a rule that overrides every other passage.
Q3. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… this power, that whoever I lay my hands on may receive the ______ Spirit."”? (8:19, WEB) Medium
A) innocent B) guilty C) Holy D) pure
Answer: C. Holy. The passage specifies Holy: “… this power, that whoever I lay my hands on may receive the Holy Spirit."”
Q4. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “… heard that Samaria had received the word of God, they sent ______ and ______ to them,” (8:14, WEB) Medium
Answer: Peter; John. The wording is “… heard that Samaria had received the word of God, they sent Peter and John to them,” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q5. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… this, your wickedness, and ask God if perhaps the thought of your heart may be forgiven you.” (8:22, WEB) Medium
Answer: True. The actual wording is “… this, your wickedness, and ask God if perhaps the thought of your heart may be forgiven you.”
Q6. Multiple choice: Which word completes “But when they believed ______ preaching good news concerning the Kingdom of God and the name of Jesus Christ, they were baptized, both men and women.”? (8:12, WEB) Easy
A) Philip B) Syntyche C) Manasseh D) Sergius
Answer: A. Philip. Acts 8:12 uses Philip: “But when they believed Philip preaching good news concerning the Kingdom of God and the name of Jesus Christ, they were baptized, both men and women.”
Q7. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “… the Ethiopians, who was over all her treasure, who had come to ______ to worship.” (8:27); “He said, "How can I, unless someone ______ it to me?" He begged Philip to come up and sit with him.” (8:31, WEB) Hard
A) explains; Jerusalem B) Troas; explains C) Jerusalem; explains D) Jerusalem; spirits
Answer: C. Jerusalem; explains. Verse 27 reads “… the Ethiopians, who was over all her treasure, who had come to Jerusalem to worship.”; verse 31 reads “He said, "How can I, unless someone explains it to me?" He begged Philip to come up and sit with him.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q8. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “But ______ ravaged the assembly, entering into every house, and dragged both men and women off to prison.” (8:3, WEB) Easy
Answer: Saul. The verse reads, “But Saul ravaged the assembly, entering into every house, and dragged both men and women off to prison.”
Acts 9
Q1. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… who heard him were amazed, and said, "Isn't this he who in ______ made havoc of those who called on this name? And he had come …”? (9:21, WEB) Medium
A) Cana B) Joppa C) Jerusalem D) Hebron
Answer: C. Jerusalem. The passage specifies Jerusalem: “… who heard him were amazed, and said, "Isn't this he who in Jerusalem made havoc of those who called on this name? And he had come …”
Q2. Multiple choice: Which word completes “and in a vision he has seen a man named ______ coming in, and laying his hands on him, that he might receive his sight."”? (9:12, WEB) Easy
A) James B) Zebulun C) Reuben D) Ananias

Q3. Multiple choice: How does Saul's encounter reshape the meaning of his persecution? (9:1-19) Hard
A) Jesus identifies persecution of His followers as persecution directed against Himself. B) The encounter leaves Saul's mission unchanged. C) Jesus praises Saul's violence as the goal of discipleship. D) Saul is told that treatment of believers never concerns Jesus.
Answer: A. Jesus identifies persecution of His followers as persecution directed against Himself. The encounter interrupts Saul and sends him into dependence on the community he had threatened. The course's picture of the church as Christ's body gives this identification particular weight.

Q6. Multiple choice: What convinces Peter that Cornelius's household should not be excluded from baptism? (10:34-48) Hard
A) Peter sees no evidence of God's action. B) The Spirit's gift is said to belong only to ethnic Jews. C) Cornelius first becomes a Jewish temple priest. D) God gives the Holy Spirit to these Gentiles as the listeners witness.
Answer: D. God gives the Holy Spirit to these Gentiles as the listeners witness. Peter recognizes God's impartial welcome and the Spirit's gift. The scene is important evidence for Gentile inclusion; it does not explicitly resolve every later question about household membership or baptismal mode.

Q3. Multiple choice: What central burden does the council refuse to impose on Gentile believers? (15:1-21) Hard
A) Every Gentile must become a Jewish proselyte before receiving grace. B) Faith in Jesus is forbidden to Gentiles. C) Circumcision and full observance of the Mosaic law as a requirement for salvation. D) Gentile believers are excluded from fellowship by definition.
Answer: C. Circumcision and full observance of the Mosaic law as a requirement for salvation. Peter speaks of the Lord Jesus' grace, and James discusses Gentile inclusion. The letter also addresses practices important to the new communities; it should be read in that historical setting.

Q2. Multiple choice: How does Paul's address at Athens use and challenge the audience's religious ideas? (17:22-31) Hard
A) Every Athenian belief is affirmed without challenge. B) He begins from their setting but calls them to the Creator, repentance and judgment through the risen man God appointed. C) Paul says the Creator needs human temples in order to exist. D) The resurrection is omitted as irrelevant.
Answer: B. He begins from their setting but calls them to the Creator, repentance and judgment through the risen man God appointed. Paul finds a point of contact without affirming every local belief. The course's concern for culture does not mean treating all claims about God as interchangeable.
Q3. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “Now while Paul waited for them at ______, his spirit was provoked within him as he saw the city full of idols.”? (17:16, WEB) Medium
A) Sinai B) Philippi C) Nazareth D) Athens
Answer: D. Athens. The passage specifies Athens: “Now while Paul waited for them at Athens, his spirit was provoked within him as he saw the city full of idols.”
Q4. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “Now all the Athenians and the strangers living there spent their time in nothing else, but either to tell or to hear some new thing.” (17:21, WEB) Medium
Answer: True. The actual wording is “Now all the Athenians and the strangers living there spent their time in nothing else, but either to tell or to hear some new thing.”
Q5. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “… of ignorance therefore God overlooked. But now he commands that all people ______ should repent,” (17:30); “… men joined with him, and believed, among whom also was Dionysius the ______, and a woman named Damaris, and others with them.” (17:34, WEB) Hard
Answer: everywhere; Areopagite. The first passage reads “… of ignorance therefore God overlooked. But now he commands that all people everywhere should repent,”; the second reads “… men joined with him, and believed, among whom also was Dionysius the Areopagite, and a woman named Damaris, and others with them.”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Q6. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… they didn't find them, they dragged Jason and certain brothers before the ______ of the city, crying, "These who have turned the world upside down have …” (17:6, WEB) Easy
Answer: rulers. The cited words are “… they didn't find them, they dragged Jason and certain brothers before the rulers of the city, crying, "These who have turned the world upside down have …”
Q7. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “explaining and demonstrating that the ______ had to suffer and rise again from the dead, and saying, "This Jesus, whom I proclaim to you, is the …” (17:3, WEB) Easy
Answer: Christ. The verse reads, “explaining and demonstrating that the Christ had to suffer and rise again from the dead, and saying, "This Jesus, whom I proclaim to you, is the …”
Q8. Multiple choice: Which word completes “The brothers immediately sent Paul and ______ away by night to Beroea. When they arrived, they went into the Jewish synagogue.”? (17:10, WEB) Easy
A) Lazarus B) Christ C) Silas D) Gaius
Answer: C. Silas. Acts 17:10 uses Silas: “The brothers immediately sent Paul and Silas away by night to Beroea. When they arrived, they went into the Jewish synagogue.”
Acts 18
Q1. Multiple choice: Which word completes “______, the ruler of the synagogue, believed in the Lord with all his house. Many of the Corinthians, when they heard, believed and were baptized.”? (18:8, WEB) Easy
A) Judas B) Priscilla C) Crispus D) Jezebel
Answer: C. Crispus. Acts 18:8 uses Crispus: “Crispus, the ruler of the synagogue, believed in the Lord with all his house. Many of the Corinthians, when they heard, believed and were baptized.”
Q2. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “He found a certain Jew named ______, a man of Pontus by race, who had recently come from Italy, with his wife Priscilla, because Claudius had …” (18:2, WEB) Easy
Answer: Aquila. The verse reads, “He found a certain Jew named Aquila, a man of Pontus by race, who had recently come from Italy, with his wife Priscilla, because Claudius had …”
Q3. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “… accurately the things concerning Jesus, although he knew only the baptism of ______.” (18:25); “… refuted the Jews, publicly showing by the Scriptures that Jesus was the ______.” (18:28, WEB) Hard
Answer: John; Christ. The first passage reads “… accurately the things concerning Jesus, although he knew only the baptism of John.”; the second reads “… refuted the Jews, publicly showing by the Scriptures that Jesus was the Christ.”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Q4. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… indeed it were a matter of wrong or of wicked crime, you ______, it would be reasonable that I should bear with you;”? (18:14, WEB) Medium
A) Greeks B) matters C) Jews D) questions
Answer: C. Jews. The passage specifies Jews: “… indeed it were a matter of wrong or of wicked crime, you Jews, it would be reasonable that I should bear with you;”
Q5. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “… leave of the brothers, and sailed from there for Syria, together with ______ and Aquila. He shaved his head in Cenchreae, for he had a vow.” (18:18); “… and saying, "I must by all means keep this coming feast in ______, but I will return again to you if God wills," he set sail …” (18:21, WEB) Hard
A) Jerusalem; Priscilla B) Priscilla; Jerusalem C) Peter; Jerusalem D) Priscilla; Sinai
Answer: B. Priscilla; Jerusalem. Verse 18 reads “… leave of the brothers, and sailed from there for Syria, together with Priscilla and Aquila. He shaved his head in Cenchreae, for he had a vow.”; verse 21 reads “… and saying, "I must by all means keep this coming feast in Jerusalem, but I will return again to you if God wills," he set sail …”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q6. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… on Sosthenes, the ruler of the synagogue, and beat him before the word seat. Gallio didn't care about any of these things.” (18:17, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “… on Sosthenes, the ruler of the synagogue, and beat him before the judgment seat. Gallio didn't care about any of these things.” It says judgment, not word.
Q7. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “But when ______ was proconsul of Achaia, the Jews with one accord rose up against ______ and brought him before the judgment seat,” (18:12, WEB) Medium
Answer: Gallio; Paul. The wording is “But when Gallio was proconsul of Achaia, the Jews with one accord rose up against Paul and brought him before the judgment seat,” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q8. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “But when Silas and Timothy came down from ______, Paul was compelled by the Spirit, testifying to the Jews that Jesus was the Christ.” (18:5, WEB) Easy
Answer: Macedonia. The cited words are “But when Silas and Timothy came down from Macedonia, Paul was compelled by the Spirit, testifying to the Jews that Jesus was the Christ.”
Acts 19
Q1. Multiple choice: What distinction does Paul make in speaking with the disciples at Ephesus? (19:1-7) Hard
A) John's message is described as denying any coming Messiah. B) He distinguishes John's baptism of repentance from faith directed toward Jesus. C) The account states that every Christian experience must have every same detail. D) Jesus is said to be irrelevant to their faith.
Answer: B. He distinguishes John's baptism of repentance from faith directed toward Jesus. The encounter concerns their incomplete understanding of the message. Its sequence is described, while Christians debate how it relates to later experiences of the Spirit and spiritual gifts.
Q2. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “For a certain man named Demetrius, a silversmith, who made silver shrines of Artemis, brought no little business to the craftsmen,” (19:24, WEB) Medium
Answer: True. The actual wording is “For a certain man named Demetrius, a silversmith, who made silver shrines of Artemis, brought no little business to the craftsmen,”
Q3. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “Paul said, "______ indeed baptized with the baptism of repentance, saying to the people that they should believe in the one who would come after him, …” (19:4, WEB) Easy
Answer: John. The verse reads, “Paul said, "John indeed baptized with the baptism of repentance, saying to the people that they should believe in the one who would come after him, …”
Q4. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “… danger that this our trade come into disrepute, but also that the ______ of the great goddess Artemis will be counted as nothing, and her majesty …” (19:27); “… him forward. Alexander beckoned with his hand, and would have made a ______ to the people.” (19:33, WEB) Hard
A) temple; forward B) temple; defense C) defense; temple D) table; defense
Answer: B. temple; defense. Verse 27 reads “… danger that this our trade come into disrepute, but also that the temple of the great goddess Artemis will be counted as nothing, and her majesty …”; verse 33 reads “… him forward. Alexander beckoned with his hand, and would have made a defense to the people.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q5. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “He entered into the synagogue, and spoke boldly for a period of ______ months, reasoning and persuading about the things concerning the Kingdom of God.” (19:8, WEB) Easy
Answer: three. The cited words are “He entered into the synagogue, and spoke boldly for a period of three months, reasoning and persuading about the things concerning the Kingdom of God.”
Q6. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… all. They counted the price of them, and found it to be ______ thousand pieces of silver.”? (19:19, WEB) Medium
A) thirty B) fifty C) four D) sixth
Answer: B. fifty. The passage specifies fifty: “… all. They counted the price of them, and found it to be fifty thousand pieces of silver.”
Q7. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “The man in whom the evil ______ was ______ on them, and overpowered them, and prevailed against them, so that they fled out of that house …” (19:16, WEB) Medium
Answer: spirit; leaped. The wording is “The man in whom the evil spirit was leaped on them, and overpowered them, and prevailed against them, so that they fled out of that house …” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q8. Multiple choice: Which word completes “This continued for two years, so that all those who lived in ______ heard the word of the Lord Jesus, both Jews and Greeks.”? (19:10, WEB) Easy
A) Berea B) Macedonia C) Nazareth D) Asia
Answer: D. Asia. Acts 19:10 uses Asia: “This continued for two years, so that all those who lived in Asia heard the word of the Lord Jesus, both Jews and Greeks.”
Acts 20
Q1. Multiple choice: How does Paul's warning about leaders who draw disciples after themselves define a danger to the church? (20:28-35) Hard
A) Concern for the weak is excluded from Paul's example. B) Every confident leader is beyond scrutiny. C) Shepherds can exploit the flock instead of caring for people entrusted to them. D) The flock exists only to enlarge a leader's status.
Answer: C. Shepherds can exploit the flock instead of caring for people entrusted to them. Paul joins vigilance over teaching with personal example and care for the weak. The course warns against churches becoming extensions of a leader's ego.
Q2. Multiple choice: Which word completes “When he had gone up, and had broken ______, and eaten, and had talked with them a long while, even until break of day, he departed.”? (20:11, WEB) Easy
A) bread B) salt C) olives D) corn
Answer: A. bread. Acts 20:11 uses bread: “When he had gone up, and had broken bread, and eaten, and had talked with them a long while, even until break of day, he departed.”
Q3. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “I coveted no one's ______, or gold, or clothing.” (20:33); “They all wept a lot, and ______ on Paul's neck and kissed him,” (20:37, WEB) Hard
Answer: silver; fell. The first passage reads “I coveted no one's silver, or gold, or clothing.”; the second reads “They all wept a lot, and fell on Paul's neck and kissed him,”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Q4. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “Sailing from there, we came the following day opposite Chios. The ______ day we ______ at Samos and stayed at Trogyllium, and the day after we …” (20:15, WEB) Medium
Answer: next; touched. The wording is “Sailing from there, we came the following day opposite Chios. The next day we touched at Samos and stayed at Trogyllium, and the day after we …” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q5. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… was about to set sail for Syria, he determined to return through ______.” (20:3, WEB) Easy
Answer: Macedonia. The verse reads, “… was about to set sail for Syria, he determined to return through Macedonia.”
Q6. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “except that the Holy Spirit testifies in every city, saying that bonds and afflictions wait for me.” (20:23, WEB) Medium
Answer: True. The actual wording is “except that the Holy Spirit testifies in every city, saying that bonds and afflictions wait for me.”
Q7. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “On the first day of the week, when the ______ were gathered together to break bread, Paul talked with them, intending to depart on the next …” (20:7, WEB) Easy
Answer: disciples. The cited words are “On the first day of the week, when the disciples were gathered together to break bread, Paul talked with them, intending to depart on the next …”
Q8. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… "You yourselves know, from the first day that I set foot in ______, how I was with you all the time,”? (20:18, WEB) Medium
A) Galilee B) Egypt C) Asia D) Thessalonica
Answer: C. Asia. The passage specifies Asia: “… "You yourselves know, from the first day that I set foot in Asia, how I was with you all the time,”

Q3. Q3. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… had passed, Agrippa the King and Bernice arrived at Caesarea, and greeted ______.”? (25:13, WEB) Medium
A) Festus B) Benjamin C) Trophimus D) Mark
Answer: A. Festus. The passage specifies Festus: “… had passed, Agrippa the King and Bernice arrived at Caesarea, and greeted Festus.”
Q4. Answer: King; Agrippa. The first passage reads “Festus said, "King Agrippa, and all men who are here present with us, you see this man, about whom all the multitude of the Jews petitioned …”; the second reads “… I have brought him forth before you, and especially before you, King Agrippa, that, after examination, I may have something to write.”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.

Q7. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “… Paul said, "I am standing before Caesar's judgment seat, where I ______ to be ______. I have done no wrong to the Jews, as you also …” (25:10, WEB) Medium

Acts 28
Q1. Multiple choice: How does the book's closing picture of Paul relate to its mission? (28:23-31) Hard
A) The book ends by saying the word can no longer be taught. B) Despite confinement, he continues proclaiming God's kingdom and teaching about Jesus. C) The closing scene claims to narrate every detail of Paul's death. D) Paul is shown abandoning Jesus' message.
Answer: B. Despite confinement, he continues proclaiming God's kingdom and teaching about Jesus. Acts ends with witness continuing in Rome rather than with an account of every later event in Paul's life. Constraint has not silenced the message.
Q2. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… Paul had gathered a bundle of sticks and laid them on the ______, a viper came out because of the heat, and fastened on his hand.” (28:3, WEB) Easy
Answer: fire. The verse reads, “… Paul had gathered a bundle of sticks and laid them on the fire, a viper came out because of the heat, and fastened on his hand.”
Q3. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… Market of Appius and The Three Taverns. When Paul saw them, he ______ God, and took courage.”? (28:15, WEB) Medium
A) expected B) thanked C) entertained D) delivered
Answer: B. thanked. The passage specifies thanked: “… Market of Appius and The Three Taverns. When Paul saw them, he thanked God, and took courage.”
Q4. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… for a long time and saw nothing bad happen to him, they ______ their minds, and said that he was a god.” (28:6, WEB) Easy
Answer: changed. The cited words are “… for a long time and saw nothing bad happen to him, they changed their minds, and said that he was a god.”
Q5. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “… the Kingdom of God, and persuading them concerning Jesus, both from the ______ of Moses and from the prophets, from morning until evening.” (28:23); “… after Paul had spoken one word, "The Holy Spirit spoke rightly through ______, the prophet, to our fathers,” (28:25, WEB) Hard
A) law; Demetrius B) law; Isaiah C) Isaiah; law D) judgment; Isaiah
Answer: B. law; Isaiah. Verse 23 reads “… the Kingdom of God, and persuading them concerning Jesus, both from the law of Moses and from the prophets, from morning until evening.”; verse 25 reads “… after Paul had spoken one word, "The Holy Spirit spoke rightly through Isaiah, the prophet, to our fathers,”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q6. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… happened that the father of Publius lay sick of fever and dysentery. ______ entered in to him, prayed, and laying his hands on him, healed him.”? (28:8, WEB) Easy
A) Paul B) Judah C) Saul D) Aaron
Answer: A. Paul. Acts 28:8 uses Paul: “… happened that the father of Publius lay sick of fever and dysentery. Paul entered in to him, prayed, and laying his hands on him, healed him.”
Q7. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “After ______ months, we set sail in a ship of Alexandria which had wintered in the island, whose sign was "The Twin ______."” (28:11, WEB) Medium
Answer: three; Brothers. The wording is “After three months, we set sail in a ship of Alexandria which had wintered in the island, whose sign was "The Twin Brothers."” The missing terms belong in that order.
