Answer: True. The actual wording is “… people, or the customs of our fathers, still was delivered prisoner from Jerusalem into the hands of the Romans,”
Romans
Romans 1
Q1. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “through whom we received ______ and apostleship, for obedience of faith among all the nations, for his name's sake;” (1:5, WEB) Easy
Answer: grace. The cited words are “through whom we received grace and apostleship, for obedience of faith among all the nations, for his name's sake;”
Q2. Multiple choice: Which word completes “For God is my ______, whom I serve in my spirit in the Gospel of his Son, how unceasingly I make mention of you always in …”? (1:9, WEB) Easy
A) covetousness B) wickedness C) witness D) ungodliness
Answer: C. witness. Romans 1:9 uses witness: “For God is my witness, whom I serve in my spirit in the Gospel of his Son, how unceasingly I make mention of you always in …”
Q3. Multiple choice: How does Paul connect the gospel with righteousness and faith? (1:16-17) Hard
A) Faith is excluded from Paul's account of righteousness. B) The gospel is limited to one ethnic people. C) The gospel is God's saving power, revealing His righteousness in relation to faith. D) Saving power is attributed to social prestige.
Answer: C. The gospel is God's saving power, revealing His righteousness in relation to faith. These verses announce themes developed throughout the letter. The course connects salvation with God's action in Christ rather than with a claim to human self-sufficiency.
Q4. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “… neither gave thanks, but became vain in their reasoning, and their senseless ______ was darkened.” (1:21); “… truth of God for a lie, and worshiped and served the creature ______ than the Creator, who is blessed forever. Amen.” (1:25, WEB) Hard
A) heart; brothers B) heart; rather C) eyes; rather D) rather; heart
Answer: B. heart; rather. Verse 21 reads “… neither gave thanks, but became vain in their reasoning, and their senseless heart was darkened.”; verse 25 reads “… truth of God for a lie, and worshiped and served the creature rather than the Creator, who is blessed forever. Amen.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q5. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… from heaven against all ungodliness and unrighteousness of men, who suppress the truth in unrighteousness,” (1:18, WEB) Medium
Answer: True. The actual wording is “… from heaven against all ungodliness and unrighteousness of men, who suppress the truth in unrighteousness,”
Q6. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “… often ______ to come to you, and was hindered so far, that I might have some fruit among you also, even as among the ______ of …” (1:13, WEB) Medium
Answer: planned; rest. The wording is “… often planned to come to you, and was hindered so far, that I might have some fruit among you also, even as among the rest of …” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q7. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… the Gospel of Christ, for it is the power of God for ______ for everyone who believes; for the Jew first, and also for the Greek.”? (1:16, WEB) Medium
A) salvation B) inheritance C) rest D) offering
Answer: A. salvation. The passage specifies salvation: “… the Gospel of Christ, for it is the power of God for salvation for everyone who believes; for the Jew first, and also for the Greek.”
Q8. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… of God with power, according to the Spirit of holiness, by the ______ from the dead, Jesus Christ our Lord,” (1:4, WEB) Easy
Answer: resurrection. The verse reads, “… of God with power, according to the Spirit of holiness, by the resurrection from the dead, Jesus Christ our Lord,”
Romans 2
Q1. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “But according to your hardness and unrepentant ______ you are treasuring up for yourself wrath in the day of wrath, revelation, and of the righteous judgment …” (2:5, WEB) Easy
Answer: heart. The cited words are “But according to your hardness and unrepentant heart you are treasuring up for yourself wrath in the day of wrath, revelation, and of the righteous judgment …”
Q2. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… who don't have the law do by nature the things of the ______, these, not having the law, are a law to themselves,”? (2:14, WEB) Medium
A) words B) law C) instruction D) judgments
Answer: B. law. The passage specifies law: “… who don't have the law do by nature the things of the law, these, not having the law, are a law to themselves,”
Q3. Multiple choice: What does the discussion of conscience add to Paul's argument about judgment? (2:1-16) Hard
A) People who judge others remain accountable, and mere possession of the law does not remove that accountability. B) Judging another automatically proves innocence. C) Having heard the law excuses every violation. D) Conscience is said to make all human choices morally identical.
Answer: A. People who judge others remain accountable, and mere possession of the law does not remove that accountability. Paul confronts moral superiority and discusses the witness of conscience. The course links conscience with moral responsibility, not a guarantee that every judgment of conscience is correct.
Q4. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… in that which you judge another, you condemn yourself. For you who ______ practice the same things.” (2:1, WEB) Easy
Answer: judge. The verse reads, “… in that which you judge another, you condemn yourself. For you who judge practice the same things.”
Q5. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “Indeed you bear the name of a Jew, and prayer on the law, and glory in God,” (2:17, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “Indeed you bear the name of a Jew, and rest on the law, and glory in God,” It says rest, not prayer.
Q6. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “But ______ and honor and ______ to every man who works good, to the Jew first, and also to the Greek.” (2:10, WEB) Medium
Answer: glory; peace. The wording is “But glory and honor and peace to every man who works good, to the Jew first, and also to the Greek.” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q7. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “… if you are a transgressor of the law, your circumcision has become ______.” (2:25); “… that of the heart, in the spirit not in the letter; whose ______ is not from men, but from God.” (2:29, WEB) Hard
Answer: uncircumcision; praise. The first passage reads “… if you are a transgressor of the law, your circumcision has become uncircumcision.”; the second reads “… that of the heart, in the spirit not in the letter; whose praise is not from men, but from God.”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Q8. Multiple choice: Which word completes “but to those who are self-seeking, and don't obey the ______, but obey unrighteousness, will be wrath and indignation,”? (2:8, WEB) Easy
A) truth B) wrath C) justice D) peace
Answer: A. truth. Romans 2:8 uses truth: “but to those who are self-seeking, and don't obey the truth, but obey unrighteousness, will be wrath and indignation,”
Romans 3
Q1. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “"Their feet are swift to shed ______.”? (3:15, WEB) Medium
A) lips B) blood C) teeth D) bone
Answer: B. blood. The passage specifies blood: “"Their feet are swift to shed blood.”
Q2. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… we previously charged both Jews and Greeks, that they are all under ______.”? (3:9, WEB) Easy
A) sin B) idolatry C) transgression D) disobedience
Answer: A. sin. Romans 3:9 uses sin: “… we previously charged both Jews and Greeks, that they are all under sin.”
Q3. Multiple choice: How are universal sin and justification by grace presented together? (3:21-28) Hard
A) All stand in need, and justification is given through God's grace in Christ rather than boasting in works of law. B) Only one ethnic group needs forgiveness. C) Boasting is the intended result of justification. D) Grace is defined as a wage earned through moral perfection.
Answer: A. All stand in need, and justification is given through God's grace in Christ rather than boasting in works of law. Paul joins the diagnosis of sin with the announcement of redemption. The course uses this to explain why salvation is good news rather than a reward for an already perfect life.
Q4. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “But if our unrighteousness commends the ______ of God, what will we say? Is God unrighteous who inflicts wrath? I speak like men do.” (3:5, WEB) Easy
Answer: righteousness. The cited words are “But if our unrighteousness commends the righteousness of God, what will we say? Is God unrighteous who inflicts wrath? I speak like men do.”
Q5. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “For what if some were without faith? Will their lack of ______ nullify the faithfulness of God?” (3:3, WEB) Easy
Answer: faith. The verse reads, “For what if some were without faith? Will their lack of faith nullify the faithfulness of God?”
Q6. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “"Their throat is an ______ tomb. With their tongues they have used deceit." "The poison of ______ is under their lips;"” (3:13, WEB) Medium
Answer: open; vipers. The wording is “"Their throat is an open tomb. With their tongues they have used deceit." "The poison of vipers is under their lips;"” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q7. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “… is the boasting? It is excluded. By what manner of law? Of ______? No, but by a law of faith.” (3:27); “since indeed there is one God who will justify the ______ by faith, and the uncircumcised through faith.” (3:30, WEB) Hard
Answer: works; circumcised. The first passage reads “… is the boasting? It is excluded. By what manner of law? Of works? No, but by a law of faith.”; the second reads “since indeed there is one God who will justify the circumcised by faith, and the uncircumcised through faith.”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Q8. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… things the law says, it speaks to those who are under the commandments, that every mouth may be closed, and all the world may be brought …” (3:19, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “… things the law says, it speaks to those who are under the law, that every mouth may be closed, and all the world may be brought …” It says law, not commandments.
Romans 4
Q1. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “Is this ______ then pronounced on the circumcised, or on the uncircumcised also? For we say that ______ was accounted to Abraham for righteousness.” (4:9, WEB) Medium
Answer: blessing; faith. The wording is “Is this blessing then pronounced on the circumcised, or on the uncircumcised also? For we say that faith was accounted to Abraham for righteousness.” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q2. Multiple choice: Why is the timing of Abraham's circumcision important to Paul's argument? (4:9-12) Hard
A) Abraham was first declared righteous after becoming a Mosaic priest. B) Circumcision occurred before every divine promise to Abraham. C) Abraham's faith was counted as righteousness before he received circumcision. D) Paul says Abraham never had any faith.
Answer: C. Abraham's faith was counted as righteousness before he received circumcision. Paul uses the sequence to explain Abraham's relation to both uncircumcised and circumcised believers. The sign does not become the prior cause of God's promise in his argument.
Q3. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… believes in him who justifies the ungodly, his faith is accounted for ______.” (4:5, WEB) Easy
Answer: righteousness. The cited words are “… believes in him who justifies the ungodly, his faith is accounted for righteousness.”
Q4. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… this cause it is of faith, that it may be according to grace, to the end that the promise may be sure to all the seed, …” (4:16, WEB) Medium
Answer: True. The actual wording is “… this cause it is of faith, that it may be according to grace, to the end that the promise may be sure to all the seed, …”
Q5. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “For if ______ was justified by works, he has something to boast about, but not toward God.” (4:2, WEB) Easy
Answer: Abraham. The verse reads, “For if Abraham was justified by works, he has something to boast about, but not toward God.”
Q6. Multiple choice: Which word completes “"Blessed are they whose ______ are forgiven, Whose sins are covered.”? (4:7, WEB) Easy
A) wickedness B) rebellion C) deceit D) iniquities
Answer: D. iniquities. Romans 4:7 uses iniquities: “"Blessed are they whose iniquities are forgiven, Whose sins are covered.”
Q7. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “For the ______ to Abraham and to his seed that he should be heir of the world wasn't through the law, but through the righteousness of …”? (4:13, WEB) Medium
A) testimony B) promise C) word D) judgment
Answer: B. promise. The passage specifies promise: “For the promise to Abraham and to his seed that he should be heir of the world wasn't through the law, but through the righteousness of …”
Q8. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “… God, he didn't waver through unbelief, but grew strong through faith, giving ______ to God,” (4:20); “… to whom it will be accounted, who believe in him who raised ______, our Lord, from the dead,” (4:24, WEB) Hard
Answer: glory; Jesus. The first passage reads “… God, he didn't waver through unbelief, but grew strong through faith, giving glory to God,”; the second reads “… to whom it will be accounted, who believe in him who raised Jesus, our Lord, from the dead,”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Romans 5
Q1. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… if, while we were enemies, we were reconciled to God through the ______ of his Son, much more, being reconciled, we will be saved by his …”? (5:10, WEB) Medium
A) offerings B) life C) death D) salvation
Answer: C. death. The passage specifies death: “… if, while we were enemies, we were reconciled to God through the death of his Son, much more, being reconciled, we will be saved by his …”
Q2. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “and hope doesn't disappoint us, because God's ______ has been poured out into our hearts through the Holy Spirit who was given to us.” (5:5, WEB) Easy
Answer: love. The cited words are “and hope doesn't disappoint us, because God's love has been poured out into our hearts through the Holy Spirit who was given to us.”
Q3. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… by faith into this grace in which we stand. We rejoice in ______ of the glory of God.” (5:2, WEB) Easy
Answer: hope. The verse reads, “… by faith into this grace in which we stand. We rejoice in hope of the glory of God.”
Q4. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “But God commends his own love toward us, in that ______ we were yet sinners, ______ died for us.” (5:8, WEB) Medium
Answer: while; Christ. The wording is “But God commends his own love toward us, in that while we were yet sinners, Christ died for us.” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q5. Multiple choice: What does Christ's death while people were still sinners reveal? (5:6-11) Hard
A) Christ's death concerns only people who have never sinned. B) God's love reaches people before they have made themselves worthy of reconciliation. C) Reconciliation is described as unnecessary. D) God waits until enemies have earned His affection.
Answer: B. God's love reaches people before they have made themselves worthy of reconciliation. Paul describes God's action toward the weak and His enemies. The course connects our responsive love with the love God first shows in Christ.
Q6. Multiple choice: Which word completes “For one will hardly die for a ______ man. Yet perhaps for a righteous person someone would even dare to die.”? (5:7, WEB) Easy
A) holy B) obedient C) righteous D) wise
Answer: C. righteous. Romans 5:7 uses righteous: “For one will hardly die for a righteous man. Yet perhaps for a righteous person someone would even dare to die.”
Q7. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… as sin entered into the world through one man, and death through rebellion; and so death passed to all men, because all sinned.” (5:12, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “… as sin entered into the world through one man, and death through sin; and so death passed to all men, because all sinned.” It says sin, not rebellion.
Q8. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “Nevertheless death reigned from Adam until ______, even over those whose sins weren't like Adam's disobedience, who is a foreshadowing of him who was to come.” (5:14); “… the abundance of grace and of the gift of righteousness reign in ______ through the one, Jesus Christ.” (5:17, WEB) Hard
A) Luke; life B) Moses; life C) Moses; worship D) life; Moses
Answer: B. Moses; life. Verse 14 reads “Nevertheless death reigned from Adam until Moses, even over those whose sins weren't like Adam's disobedience, who is a foreshadowing of him who was to come.”; verse 17 reads “… the abundance of grace and of the gift of righteousness reign in life through the one, Jesus Christ.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Romans 6
Q1. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… also to be dead to sin, but alive to God in Christ ______ our Lord.”? (6:11, WEB) Medium
A) Timothy B) Jesus C) Herod D) Crispus
Answer: B. Jesus. The passage specifies Jesus: “… also to be dead to sin, but alive to God in Christ Jesus our Lord.”
Q2. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… God, as alive from the dead, and your members as instruments of faithfulness to God.” (6:13, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “… God, as alive from the dead, and your members as instruments of righteousness to God.” It says righteousness, not faithfulness.
Q3. Multiple choice: Why does Paul reject continuing in sin as a way to increase grace? (6:1-14) Hard
A) Resurrection has no relation to daily conduct. B) Paul denies that sin can still be resisted. C) Union with Christ's death and resurrection calls believers into a new life. D) Grace requires believers to cultivate more sin.
Answer: C. Union with Christ's death and resurrection calls believers into a new life. Grace is not permission to preserve sin's rule. The course distinguishes receiving forgiveness from using forgiveness as an excuse to avoid transformation.
Q4. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… glory of the Father, so we also might walk in newness of ______.” (6:4, WEB) Easy
Answer: life. The verse reads, “… glory of the Father, so we also might walk in newness of life.”
Q5. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “knowing that ______, being raised from the dead, dies no more. ______ no more has dominion over him!” (6:9, WEB) Medium
Answer: Christ; Death. The wording is “knowing that Christ, being raised from the dead, dies no more. Death no more has dominion over him!” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q6. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “… know that to whom you present yourselves as servants to obedience, his ______ you are whom you obey; whether of sin to death, or of obedience …” (6:16); “… that, whereas you were bondservants of sin, you became obedient from the ______ to that form of teaching whereunto you were delivered.” (6:17, WEB) Hard
A) servants; bone B) servants; heart C) heart; servants D) wife; heart
Answer: B. servants; heart. Verse 16 reads “… know that to whom you present yourselves as servants to obedience, his servants you are whom you obey; whether of sin to death, or of obedience …”; verse 17 reads “… that, whereas you were bondservants of sin, you became obedient from the heart to that form of teaching whereunto you were delivered.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q7. Multiple choice: Which word completes “For he who has died has been freed from ______.”? (6:7, WEB) Easy
A) iniquities B) sin C) transgressions D) idolatry
Answer: B. sin. Romans 6:7 uses sin: “For he who has died has been freed from sin.”
Q8. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “knowing this, that our old man was crucified with him, that the ______ of sin might be done away with, so that we would no longer …” (6:6, WEB) Easy
Answer: body. The cited words are “knowing this, that our old man was crucified with him, that the body of sin might be done away with, so that we would no longer …”
Romans 7
Q1. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… the law sin? May it never be! However, I wouldn't have known ______, except through the law. For I wouldn't have known coveting, unless the law …”? (7:7, WEB) Easy
A) transgression B) sin C) iniquity D) sins
Answer: B. sin. Romans 7:7 uses sin: “… the law sin? May it never be! However, I wouldn't have known sin, except through the law. For I wouldn't have known coveting, unless the law …”
Q2. Multiple choice: What should be acknowledged when identifying the I in Paul's description of inner conflict? (7:14-25) Hard
A) The conclusion says no deliverance is needed. B) Christians interpret the speaker's situation differently, while the passage exposes the inability of the self to find deliverance in itself. C) All interpreters identify the speaker identically. D) The passage clearly teaches that God created the body as evil.
Answer: B. Christians interpret the speaker's situation differently, while the passage exposes the inability of the self to find deliverance in itself. Readers debate whether the account primarily describes a believer, life under law or a representative voice. The course also warns against turning flesh and spirit into a claim that the physical body itself is evil.
Q3. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… my brothers, you also were made dead to the law through the ______ of Christ, that you would be joined to another, to him who was …” (7:4, WEB) Easy
Answer: body. The cited words are “… my brothers, you also were made dead to the law through the body of Christ, that you would be joined to another, to him who was …”
Q4. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… he lives, but if the husband dies, she is discharged from the ______ of the husband.” (7:2, WEB) Easy
Answer: law. The verse reads, “… he lives, but if the husband dies, she is discharged from the law of the husband.”
Q5. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “… members, warring against the law of my mind, and bringing me into ______ under the law of sin which is in my members.” (7:23); “I thank God through Jesus ______, our Lord! So then with the mind, I myself serve God's law, but with the flesh, the sin's law.” (7:25, WEB) Hard
Answer: captivity; Christ. The first passage reads “… members, warring against the law of my mind, and bringing me into captivity under the law of sin which is in my members.”; the second reads “I thank God through Jesus Christ, our Lord! So then with the mind, I myself serve God's law, but with the flesh, the sin's law.”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Q6. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “I was alive apart from the law once, but when the ______ came, sin ______, and I died.” (7:9, WEB) Medium
Answer: commandment; revived. The wording is “I was alive apart from the law once, but when the commandment came, sin revived, and I died.” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q7. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… don't know what I am doing. For I don't practice what I desire to do; but what I hate, that I do.” (7:15, WEB) Medium
Answer: True. The actual wording is “… don't know what I am doing. For I don't practice what I desire to do; but what I hate, that I do.”
Q8. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “Did then that which is good become ______ to me? May it never be! But sin, that it might be shown to be sin, by working …”? (7:13, WEB) Medium
A) offering B) blessing C) death D) offerings
Answer: C. death. The passage specifies death: “Did then that which is good become death to me? May it never be! But sin, that it might be shown to be sin, by working …”
Romans 8
Q1. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… the Spirit, even we ourselves groan within ourselves, waiting for adoption, the redemption of our body.” (8:23, WEB) Medium
Answer: True. The actual wording is “… the Spirit, even we ourselves groan within ourselves, waiting for adoption, the redemption of our body.”
Q2. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… of this present time are not worthy to be compared with the ______ which will be revealed toward us.”? (8:18, WEB) Medium
A) glory B) shame C) joy D) hope
Answer: A. glory. The passage specifies glory: “… of this present time are not worthy to be compared with the glory which will be revealed toward us.”
Q3. Multiple choice: How does the promise of God's work for good fit the chapter's treatment of suffering? (8:18-30) Hard
A) The promised good is defined only as material wealth. B) Every event is said to feel immediately pleasant. C) It gives hope amid groaning and points toward conformity to the Son. D) Believers and creation are said never to groan.
Answer: C. It gives hope amid groaning and points toward conformity to the Son. Paul includes suffering and creation's longing rather than denying them. The course does not read this as a promise that every event is pleasant or that believers avoid pain.
Q4. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “… as we ought. But the Spirit himself makes intercession for us with ______ which can't be uttered.” (8:26); “… image of his Son, that he might be the firstborn among many ______.” (8:29, WEB) Hard
A) brothers; groanings B) groanings; brothers C) Spirit's; brothers D) groanings; rulers
Answer: B. groanings; brothers. Verse 26 reads “… as we ought. But the Spirit himself makes intercession for us with groanings which can't be uttered.”; verse 29 reads “… image of his Son, that he might be the firstborn among many brothers.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q5. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “because the mind of the flesh is ______ towards God; for it is not subject to God's law, neither indeed can it be.” (8:7, WEB) Easy
Answer: hostile. The cited words are “because the mind of the flesh is hostile towards God; for it is not subject to God's law, neither indeed can it be.”
Q6. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… he who raised up Christ Jesus from the dead will also give ______ to your mortal bodies through his Spirit who dwells in you.”? (8:11, WEB) Easy
A) deliverance B) life C) redemption D) prayer
Answer: B. life. Romans 8:11 uses life: “… he who raised up Christ Jesus from the dead will also give life to your mortal bodies through his Spirit who dwells in you.”
Q7. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “For you didn't receive the spirit of bondage again to ______, but you received the ______ of adoption, by whom we cry, "Abba! Father!"” (8:15, WEB) Medium
Answer: fear; Spirit. The wording is “For you didn't receive the spirit of bondage again to fear, but you received the Spirit of adoption, by whom we cry, "Abba! Father!"” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q8. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… the Spirit of life in Christ Jesus made me free from the ______ of sin and of death.” (8:2, WEB) Easy
Answer: law. The verse reads, “… the Spirit of life in Christ Jesus made me free from the law of sin and of death.”
Romans 9
Q1. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… you to reply against God? Will the thing formed ask him who published it, "Why did you make me like this?"” (9:20, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “… you to reply against God? Will the thing formed ask him who formed it, "Why did you make me like this?"” It says formed, not published.
Q2. Multiple choice: Which word completes “For this is a word of ______, "At the appointed time I will come, and Sarah will have a son."”? (9:9, WEB) Easy
A) promise B) teaching C) covenant D) words
Answer: A. promise. Romans 9:9 uses promise: “For this is a word of promise, "At the appointed time I will come, and Sarah will have a son."”
Q3. Multiple choice: What is an appropriate distinction in reading Paul's discussion of election? (9:6-24) Hard
A) God's freedom and mercy are explicit themes, while Christian accounts of their relation to human response differ. B) Every later theological school interprets this chapter identically. C) Paul says ancestry alone guarantees the fulfillment of every promise. D) The chapter avoids any discussion of mercy.
Answer: A. God's freedom and mercy are explicit themes, while Christian accounts of their relation to human response differ. The course discusses sovereignty and responsibility but rejects some Calvinist conclusions. A quiz should identify Paul's argument without treating one later system as the only possible reading of every detail.
Q4. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… you to be raised up, that I might show in you my ______, and that my name might be published abroad in all the earth."”? (9:17, WEB) Medium
A) deliverance B) salvation C) power D) worship
Answer: C. power. The passage specifies power: “… you to be raised up, that I might show in you my power, and that my name might be published abroad in all the earth."”
Q5. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “For I could wish that I myself were accursed from ______ for my brothers' sake, my relatives according to the flesh,” (9:3, WEB) Easy
Answer: Christ. The verse reads, “For I could wish that I myself were accursed from Christ for my brothers' sake, my relatives according to the flesh,”
Q6. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “Even as it is written, "______ I loved, but ______ I hated."” (9:13, WEB) Medium
Answer: Jacob; Esau. The wording is “Even as it is written, "Jacob I loved, but Esau I hated."” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q7. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… come to nothing. For they are not all Israel, that are of ______.” (9:6, WEB) Easy
Answer: Israel. The cited words are “… come to nothing. For they are not all Israel, that are of Israel.”
Q8. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “… his wrath, and to make his power known, endured with much patience ______ of wrath made for destruction,” (9:22); “______ cries concerning Israel, "If the number of the children of Israel are as the sand of the sea, It is the remnant who will be …” (9:27, WEB) Hard
A) vessels; Luke B) Isaiah; vessels C) vessels; Isaiah D) runs; Isaiah
Answer: C. vessels; Isaiah. Verse 22 reads “… his wrath, and to make his power known, endured with much patience vessels of wrath made for destruction,”; verse 27 reads “Isaiah cries concerning Israel, "If the number of the children of Israel are as the sand of the sea, It is the remnant who will be …”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.

Q4. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… the righteousness which is of faith says this, "Don't say in your ______, 'Who will ascend into heaven?' (that is, to bring Christ down);”? (10:6, WEB) Easy
A) heart B) neck C) bones D) hands
Answer: A. heart. Romans 10:6 uses heart: “… the righteousness which is of faith says this, "Don't say in your heart, 'Who will ascend into heaven?' (that is, to bring Christ down);”
Q5. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “But I ask, didn't ______ know? First Moses says, "I will provoke you to jealousy with that which is no nation, With a nation void of …” (10:19); “… day long I stretched out my hands to a disobedient and contrary ______."” (10:21, WEB) Hard

Answer: righteousness. The verse reads, “For being ignorant of God's righteousness, and seeking to establish their own righteousness, they didn't subject themselves to the righteousness of God.”
Q7. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… one believes unto righteousness; and with the mouth confession is made unto ______.”? (10:10, WEB) Medium
A) prayer B) salvation C) life D) rest
Answer: B. salvation. The passage specifies salvation: “… one believes unto righteousness; and with the mouth confession is made unto salvation.”
Q8. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “But they didn't all listen to the glad news. For ______ says, "Lord, who has believed our report?"” (10:16); “… they hear? Yes, most assuredly, "Their sound went out into all the ______, Their words to the ends of the world."” (10:18, WEB) Hard
A) Isaiah; earth B) Isaiah; mountain C) earth; Isaiah D) Jonathan; earth

Yes, most assuredly, "Their sound went out into all the earth, Their words to the ends of the world."”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Romans 11
Q1. Multiple choice: How does the olive-tree image warn Gentile believers against pride? (11:17-24) Hard
A) The branches are said to create their own root. B) Gentiles are told to despise Jewish people. C) They are supported by the root and must not boast over the branches. D) Paul makes boasting the proper response to mercy.
Answer: C. They are supported by the root and must not boast over the branches. Paul frames inclusion as a reason for humility, not contempt for Jewish people. Christian disagreements about Israel's future do not cancel this explicit warning against arrogance.
Q2. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… broken off, and you stand by your faith. Don't be conceited, but fear;” (11:20, WEB) Medium
Answer: True. The actual wording is “… broken off, and you stand by your faith. Don't be conceited, but fear;”
Q3. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “But if some of the branches were broken ______, and you, being a wild olive, were grafted in among them, and became partaker with them of …”? (11:17, WEB) Medium
A) off B) Baal C) fathers D) glory
Answer: A. off. The passage specifies off: “But if some of the branches were broken off, and you, being a wild olive, were grafted in among them, and became partaker with them of …”
Q4. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… that they might fall? May it never be! But by their fall ______ has come to the Gentiles, to provoke them to jealousy.”? (11:11, WEB) Easy
A) offering B) rest C) thanksgiving D) salvation
Answer: D. salvation. Romans 11:11 uses salvation: “… that they might fall? May it never be! But by their fall salvation has come to the Gentiles, to provoke them to jealousy.”
Q5. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “… you who are Gentiles. Since then as I am an ______ to Gentiles, I ______ my ministry;” (11:13, WEB) Medium
Answer: apostle; glorify. The wording is “… you who are Gentiles. Since then as I am an apostle to Gentiles, I glorify my ministry;” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q6. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… what the Scripture says about Elijah? How he pleads with God against ______:” (11:2, WEB) Easy
Answer: Israel. The verse reads, “… what the Scripture says about Elijah? How he pleads with God against Israel:”
Q7. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “Oh the depth of the riches both of the ______ and the knowledge of God! How unsearchable are his judgments, and his ways past tracing out!” (11:33); “"Or who has ______ given to him, And it will be repaid to him again?"” (11:35, WEB) Hard
Answer: wisdom; first. The first passage reads “Oh the depth of the riches both of the wisdom and the knowledge of God! How unsearchable are his judgments, and his ways past tracing out!”; the second reads “"Or who has first given to him, And it will be repaid to him again?"”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Q8. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… then it is no longer of works; otherwise grace is no longer ______. But if it is of works, it is no longer grace; otherwise work …” (11:6, WEB) Easy
Answer: grace. The cited words are “… then it is no longer of works; otherwise grace is no longer grace. But if it is of works, it is no longer grace; otherwise work …”
Romans 12
Q1. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “… let him do it with liberality; he who rules, with ______; he who shows ______, with cheerfulness.” (12:8, WEB) Medium
Answer: diligence; mercy. The wording is “… let him do it with liberality; he who rules, with diligence; he who shows mercy, with cheerfulness.” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q2. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “For I say, through the ______ that was given me, to every man who is among you, not to think of himself more highly than he …” (12:3, WEB) Easy
Answer: grace. The cited words are “For I say, through the grace that was given me, to every man who is among you, not to think of himself more highly than he …”
Q3. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “In ______ of the brothers be tenderly affectionate one to another; in honor preferring one another;”? (12:10, WEB) Medium
A) glory B) righteousness C) anger D) love
Answer: D. love. The passage specifies love: “In love of the brothers be tenderly affectionate one to another; in honor preferring one another;”
Q4. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “rejoicing in hope; enduring in troubles; continuing steadfastly in prayer;” (12:12, WEB) Medium
Answer: True. The actual wording is “rejoicing in hope; enduring in troubles; continuing steadfastly in prayer;”
Q5. Multiple choice: How does the appeal to God's mercies connect worship with ordinary life? (12:1-8) Hard
A) Every member is required to have an identical function. B) Believers offer themselves to God, undergo renewed thinking and serve as members of one body. C) Worship concerns only words spoken at meetings. D) The physical life is excluded from service to God.
Answer: B. Believers offer themselves to God, undergo renewed thinking and serve as members of one body. The course joins right belief with right practice. Paul's account includes embodied service and humility rather than treating worship as detached from daily conduct.
Q6. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… be transformed by the renewing of your mind, so that you may ______ what is the good, well-pleasing, and perfect will of God.” (12:2, WEB) Easy
Answer: prove. The verse reads, “… be transformed by the renewing of your mind, so that you may prove what is the good, well-pleasing, and perfect will of God.”
Q7. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “… of the same mind one toward another. Don't set your mind on ______ things, but associate with the humble. Don't be wise in your own conceits.” (12:16); “Repay no one evil for evil. Respect what is ______ in the sight of all men.” (12:17, WEB) Hard
A) honorable; high B) high; peace C) feed; honorable D) high; honorable
Answer: D. high; honorable. Verse 16 reads “… of the same mind one toward another. Don't set your mind on high things, but associate with the humble. Don't be wise in your own conceits.”; verse 17 reads “Repay no one evil for evil. Respect what is honorable in the sight of all men.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q8. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… us, if prophecy, let us prophesy according to the proportion of our ______;”? (12:6, WEB) Easy
A) heap B) thirsty C) faith D) needs
Answer: C. faith. Romans 12:6 uses faith: “… us, if prophecy, let us prophesy according to the proportion of our faith;”
Romans 13
Q1. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “For the law, "You shall not commit adultery," "You shall not murder," "You shall not steal," "You shall not give false testimony," "You shall not covet," …” (13:9, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “For the commandments, "You shall not commit adultery," "You shall not murder," "You shall not steal," "You shall not give false testimony," "You shall not covet," …” It says commandments, not law.
Q2. Multiple choice: Why should submission to governing authorities be read within the chapter's account of their purpose? (13:1-10) Hard
A) Authorities are praised specifically for rewarding evil. B) Every official action becomes morally right by definition. C) Authorities are described as servants for good, and the discussion continues with the obligation to love. D) The chapter excludes love of neighbor from public conduct.
Answer: C. Authorities are described as servants for good, and the discussion continues with the obligation to love. Christians debate applications under unjust rule. This passage should not be used to make every governmental action morally right or to erase other biblical examples of obeying God rather than people.
Q3. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “For this ______ you also pay taxes, for they are ministers of God's service, ______ continually on this very thing.” (13:6, WEB) Medium
Answer: reason; attending. The wording is “For this reason you also pay taxes, for they are ministers of God's service, attending continually on this very thing.” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q4. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… therefore to everyone what you owe: taxes to whom taxes are due; ______ to whom customs; respect to whom respect; honor to whom honor.”? (13:7, WEB) Medium
A) customs B) lusts C) Let's D) authorities
Answer: A. customs. The passage specifies customs: “… therefore to everyone what you owe: taxes to whom taxes are due; customs to whom customs; respect to whom respect; honor to whom honor.”
Q5. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… authorities, for there is no authority except from God, and those who ______ are ordained by God.” (13:1, WEB) Easy
Answer: exist. The verse reads, “… authorities, for there is no authority except from God, and those who exist are ordained by God.”
Q6. Multiple choice: Which word completes “for he is a ______ of God to you for good. But if you do that which is evil, be afraid, for he doesn't bear the …”? (13:4, WEB) Easy
A) wife B) servant C) son D) priests
Answer: B. servant. Romans 13:4 uses servant: “for he is a servant of God to you for good. But if you do that which is evil, be afraid, for he doesn't bear the …”
Q7. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “______ doesn't harm a neighbor. Love therefore is the fulfillment of the law.” (13:10); “… it is already time for you to awaken out of sleep, for ______ is now nearer to us than when we first believed.” (13:11, WEB) Hard
A) Love; salvation B) salvation; Love C) honor; salvation D) Love; life
Answer: A. Love; salvation. Verse 10 reads “Love doesn't harm a neighbor. Love therefore is the fulfillment of the law.”; verse 11 reads “… it is already time for you to awaken out of sleep, for salvation is now nearer to us than when we first believed.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q8. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… good work, but to the evil. Do you desire to have no ______ of the authority? Do that which is good, and you will have praise …” (13:3, WEB) Easy
Answer: fear. The cited words are “… good work, but to the evil. Do you desire to have no fear of the authority? Do that which is good, and you will have praise …”
Romans 14
Q1. Multiple choice: What limits does love place on the exercise of freedom over disputed practices? (14:1-23) Hard
A) Believers must avoid contempt and stumbling others while remaining accountable to the Lord. B) Every disagreement proves the other person is outside the faith. C) Freedom makes another believer's conscience irrelevant. D) The strong are commanded to display contempt.
Answer: A. Believers must avoid contempt and stumbling others while remaining accountable to the Lord. The examples concern food and days among believers. Paul does not make every ethical issue a matter of indifference; he addresses how convictions and love shape fellowship here.
Q2. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “Who are you who judge another's ______? To his own lord he stands or falls. Yes, he will be made to stand, for God has power …” (14:4, WEB) Easy
Answer: servant. The cited words are “Who are you who judge another's servant? To his own lord he stands or falls. Yes, he will be made to stand, for God has power …”
Q3. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “For it is ______, "'As I live,' says the Lord, 'to me every knee will bow. Every tongue will confess to God.'"”? (14:11, WEB) Medium
A) written B) glory C) Christ D) eternal
Answer: A. written. The passage specifies written: “For it is written, "'As I live,' says the Lord, 'to me every knee will bow. Every tongue will confess to God.'"”
Q4. Multiple choice: Which word completes “He who observes the day, ______ it to the Lord; and he who does not observe the day, to the Lord he does not observe it. …”? (14:6, WEB) Easy
A) observes B) vegetables C) food's D) esteems
Answer: A. observes. Romans 14:6 uses observes: “He who observes the day, observes it to the Lord; and he who does not observe the day, to the Lord he does not observe it. …”
Q5. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “I know, and am persuaded in the Lord Esther, that nothing is unclean of itself; except that to him who considers anything to be unclean, to …” (14:14, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “I know, and am persuaded in the Lord Jesus, that nothing is unclean of itself; except that to him who considers anything to be unclean, to …” It says Jesus, not Esther.
Q6. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… eats despise him who doesn't eat. Don't let him who doesn't eat ______ him who eats, for God has received him.” (14:3, WEB) Easy
Answer: judge. The verse reads, “… eats despise him who doesn't eat. Don't let him who doesn't eat judge him who eats, for God has received him.”
Q7. A) merciful; work B) Holy; work C) work; Holy D) Holy; slandered

All things indeed are clean, however it is evil for that man who creates a stumbling block by eating.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.

Answer: Christ; living. The wording is “For to this end Christ died, rose, and lived again, that he might be Lord of both the dead and the living.” The missing terms belong in that order.

1 Corinthians
1 Corinthians 1
Q1. Multiple choice: How does Paul's account of the cross challenge factional boasting? (1:10-31) Hard
A) Paul encourages each faction to make its leader the savior. B) The cross is treated as irrelevant to community divisions. C) Prestige is the ground of every believer's acceptance. D) God's saving work in Christ overturns confidence in status and human prestige.
Answer: D. God's saving work in Christ overturns confidence in status and human prestige. The congregation's divisions are answered by the message of the crucified Christ. The course critiques making the church an extension of a human leader's importance.
Q2. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… you be perfected together in the same mind and in the same ______.”? (1:10, WEB) Easy
A) statutes B) testimonies C) judgment D) judgments
Answer: C. judgment. 1 Corinthians 1:10 uses judgment: “… you be perfected together in the same mind and in the same judgment.”
Q3. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… saints, with all who call on the name of our Lord Jesus ______ in every place, both theirs and ours:” (1:2, WEB) Easy
Answer: Christ. The verse reads, “… saints, with all who call on the name of our Lord Jesus Christ in every place, both theirs and ours:”
Q4. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “… I mean this, that each one of you says, "I follow ______," "I follow ______," "I follow Cephas," and, "I follow Christ."” (1:12, WEB) Medium
Answer: Paul; Apollos. The wording is “… I mean this, that each one of you says, "I follow Paul," "I follow Apollos," "I follow Cephas," and, "I follow Christ."” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q5. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “… chose the weak things of the world, that he might put to ______ the things that are strong;” (1:27); “… in Christ Jesus, who was made to us wisdom from God, and ______ and sanctification, and redemption:” (1:30, WEB) Hard
Answer: shame; righteousness. The first passage reads “… chose the weak things of the world, that he might put to shame the things that are strong;”; the second reads “… in Christ Jesus, who was made to us wisdom from God, and righteousness and sanctification, and redemption:”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Q6. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “For the word of the cross is foolishness to those who are dying, but to us who are saved it is the power of God.” (1:18, WEB) Medium
Answer: True. The actual wording is “For the word of the cross is foolishness to those who are dying, but to us who are saved it is the power of God.”
Q7. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… come behind in no gift; waiting for the revelation of our Lord ______ Christ;” (1:7, WEB) Easy
Answer: Jesus. The cited words are “… come behind in no gift; waiting for the revelation of our Lord Jesus Christ;”
Q8. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “For Christ sent me not to baptize, but to preach the ______--not in wisdom of words, so that the cross of Christ wouldn't be made void.”? (1:17, WEB) Medium
A) Gospel B) shame C) Sosthenes D) weakness
Answer: A. Gospel. The passage specifies Gospel: “For Christ sent me not to baptize, but to preach the Gospel--not in wisdom of words, so that the cross of Christ wouldn't be made void.”

Q6. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “For who among men ______ the things of a man, except the spirit of the man, which is in him? Q7. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “… are foolishness to him, and he can't know them, because they are ______ discerned.” (2:14); “"For who has known the ______ of the Lord, that he should instruct him?" But we have Christ's mind.” (2:16, WEB) Hard
Answer: spiritually; mind. The first passage reads “… are foolishness to him, and he can't know them, because they are spiritually discerned.”; the second reads “"For who has known the mind of the Lord, that he should instruct him?" But we have Christ's mind.”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.

Answer: testimony. The verse reads, “… come with excellence of speech or of wisdom, proclaiming to you the testimony of God.”
1 Corinthians 3
Q1. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “But if anyone builds on the foundation with gold, ______, costly stones, wood, hay, or stubble;”? (3:12, WEB) Medium
A) stone B) sand C) silver D) stones
Answer: C. silver. The passage specifies silver: “But if anyone builds on the foundation with gold, silver, costly stones, wood, hay, or stubble;”
Q2. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “According to the ______ of God which was given to me, as a wise ______ builder I laid a foundation, and another builds on it. But …” (3:10, WEB) Medium
Answer: grace; master. The wording is “According to the grace of God which was given to me, as a wise master builder I laid a foundation, and another builds on it. But …” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q3. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “For when one says, "I follow Paul," and another, "I follow ______," aren't you fleshly?” (3:4, WEB) Easy
Answer: Apollos. The cited words are “For when one says, "I follow Paul," and another, "I follow Apollos," aren't you fleshly?”

Q5. Multiple choice: Which word completes “I ______. Apollos watered. But God gave the increase.”? (3:6, WEB) Easy
A) believed B) revealed C) planted D) watered
Answer: C. planted. 1 Corinthians 3:6 uses planted: “I planted. Apollos watered. But God gave the increase.”
Q6. Multiple choice: What does the planting-and-watering image say about the roles of Paul and Apollos? (3:5-9) Hard
A) The image makes rivalry the aim of ministry. B) Servants are to replace God as the owner of the field. C) They are servants with distinct tasks, while God gives the growth. D) Each worker produces life independently of God.
Answer: C. They are servants with distinct tasks, while God gives the growth. Paul reduces rivalry without making faithful labor meaningless. The community belongs to God rather than to competing personalities.
Q7. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “whether Paul, or Apollos, or Cephas, or the world, or life, or ______, or things present, or things to come. All are yours,” (3:22); “and you are Christ's, and ______ is God's.” (3:23, WEB) Hard
Answer: death; Christ. The first passage reads “whether Paul, or Apollos, or Cephas, or the world, or life, or death, or things present, or things to come. All are yours,”; the second reads “and you are Christ's, and Christ is God's.”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Q8. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… Day will declare it, because it is revealed in fire; and the craftiness itself will test what sort of work each man's work is.” (3:13, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “… Day will declare it, because it is revealed in fire; and the fire itself will test what sort of work each man's work is.” It says fire, not craftiness.

1 Corinthians 6
Q1. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “If then, you have to judge things pertaining to this ______, do you set them to judge who are of no account in the assembly?” (6:4, WEB) Easy
Answer: life. The cited words are “If then, you have to judge things pertaining to this life, do you set them to judge who are of no account in the assembly?”
Q2. Multiple choice: How does Paul connect bodily conduct with belonging to Christ? (6:12-20) Hard
A) Freedom means every use of the body is beneficial. B) The body has no relation to discipleship. C) The body belongs to the Lord and is described as a temple of the Holy Spirit. D) Resurrection concerns no embodied life at all.
Answer: C. The body belongs to the Lord and is described as a temple of the Holy Spirit. The passage refuses to treat bodily choices as spiritually irrelevant. The course opposes the dualist idea that only the spirit matters while the body is disposable or inherently evil.
Q3. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “nor thieves, nor covetous, nor drunkards, nor ______, nor extortioners, will inherit the Kingdom of God.”? (6:10, WEB) Medium
A) unbelievers B) sins C) foods D) slanderers
Answer: D. slanderers. The passage specifies slanderers: “nor thieves, nor covetous, nor drunkards, nor slanderers, nor extortioners, will inherit the Kingdom of God.”
Q4. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “… know that the unrighteous will not inherit the ______ of God? Don't be deceived. Neither the ______ immoral, nor idolaters, nor adulterers, nor male prostitutes, nor …” (6:9, WEB) Medium
Answer: Kingdom; sexually. The wording is “… know that the unrighteous will not inherit the Kingdom of God? Don't be deceived. Neither the sexually immoral, nor idolaters, nor adulterers, nor male prostitutes, nor …” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q5. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… the world? And if the world is judged by you, are you ______ to judge the smallest matters?” (6:2, WEB) Easy
Answer: unworthy. The verse reads, “… the world? And if the world is judged by you, are you unworthy to judge the smallest matters?”
Q6. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… have lawsuits one with another. Why not rather be wronged? Why not ______ be defrauded?”? (6:7, WEB) Easy
A) pertain B) rather C) temple D) decide
Answer: B. rather. 1 Corinthians 6:7 uses rather: “… have lawsuits one with another. Why not rather be wronged? Why not rather be defrauded?”
Q7. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “Flee sexual immorality! "Every ______ that a man does is outside the body," but he who commits sexual immorality sins against his own body.” (6:18); “… with a price. Therefore glorify God in your body and in your ______, which are God's.” (6:20, WEB) Hard
Answer: sin; spirit. The first passage reads “Flee sexual immorality! "Every sin that a man does is outside the body," but he who commits sexual immorality sins against his own body.”; the second reads “… with a price. Therefore glorify God in your body and in your spirit, which are God's.”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Q8. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… for sexual immorality, but for the Lord; and the Lord for the body.” (6:13, WEB) Medium
Answer: True. The actual wording is “… for sexual immorality, but for the Lord; and the Lord for the body.”
1 Corinthians 7

A) else B) self C) Satan D) attend
Answer: A. else. 1 Corinthians 7:11 uses else: “(but if she departs, let her remain unmarried, or else be reconciled to her husband), and that the husband not leave his wife.”
Q2. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “The wife doesn't have authority over her own ______, but the husband. Likewise also the husband doesn't have authority over his own body, but the wife.” (7:4, WEB) Easy
Answer: body. The verse reads, “The wife doesn't have authority over her own body, but the husband. Likewise also the husband doesn't have authority over his own body, but the wife.”
Q3. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “… brother or the sister is not under ______ in such cases, but God has called us in ______.” (7:15, WEB) Medium
Answer: bondage; peace. The wording is “… brother or the sister is not under bondage in such cases, but God has called us in peace.” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q4. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “Was anyone called having been ______? Let him not become uncircumcised. Has anyone been called in uncircumcision? Let him not be circumcised.”? (7:18, WEB) Medium
A) distributed B) sanctified C) reconciled D) circumcised
Answer: D. circumcised. The passage specifies circumcised: “Was anyone called having been circumcised? Let him not become uncircumcised. Has anyone been called in uncircumcision? Let him not be circumcised.”
Q5. Multiple choice: How does Paul's discussion of marriage and singleness resist one simple ranking of all Christians? (7:1-9, 25-35) Hard
A) He recognizes different gifts and addresses practical concerns about devoted service. B) Only married believers can serve God. C) Every believer is said to receive exactly the same gift. D) Only unmarried people have human dignity.
Answer: A. He recognizes different gifts and addresses practical concerns about devoted service. Paul values faithfulness in differing circumstances. Later traditions discuss some applications, but the passage does not make marriage or singleness proof of greater human worth.
Q6. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “Are you bound to a wife? Don't seek to be ______. Are you free from a wife? Don't seek a wife.” (7:27); “… as not using it to the fullest. For the mode of this ______ passes away.” (7:31, WEB) Hard
A) freed; husband B) owed; world C) freed; world D) world; freed
Answer: C. freed; world. Verse 27 reads “Are you bound to a wife? Don't seek to be freed. Are you free from a wife? Don't seek a wife.”; verse 31 reads “… as not using it to the fullest. For the mode of this world passes away.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q7. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “For he who was called in the Lord being a bound is the Lord's free man. Likewise he who was called being free is Christ's bondservant.” (7:22, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “For he who was called in the Lord being a bondservant is the Lord's free man. Likewise he who was called being free is Christ's bondservant.” It says bondservant, not bound.
Q8. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… However each man has his own gift from God, one of this ______, and another of that kind.” (7:7, WEB) Easy
Answer: kind. The cited words are “… However each man has his own gift from God, one of this kind, and another of that kind.”

Answer: D. idols. 1 Corinthians 8:4 uses idols: “Therefore concerning the eating of things sacrificed to idols, we know that no idol is anything in the world, and that there is no other God …”

Answer: doesn't. The cited words are “But if anyone thinks that he knows anything, he doesn't yet know as he ought to know.”

Q4. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… idols: We know that we all have knowledge. Knowledge puffs up, but ______ builds up.” (8:1, WEB) Easy
Answer: love. The verse reads, “… idols: We know that we all have knowledge. Knowledge puffs up, but love builds up.”
Q5. Multiple choice: What is the danger of knowledge that ignores a vulnerable believer? (8:1-13) Hard
A) The weaker believer is to be treated with contempt. B) Knowledge automatically excuses every harmful action. C) Love requires affirming that idols are true gods. D) Correct information can be used without the love that builds others up.
Answer: D. Correct information can be used without the love that builds others up. Paul distinguishes knowing about idols from loving conduct toward another person. The course's link between doctrine and practice helps explain why being factually right does not excuse harm.
Q6. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “… that by no means does this liberty of yours become a stumbling ______ to the weak.” (8:9); “… your knowledge, he who is weak perishes, the brother for whose sake ______ died.” (8:11, WEB) Hard

Answer: B. food. The passage specifies food: “But food will not commend us to God. For neither, if we don't eat, are we the worse; nor, if we eat, are we the better.”

Q2. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… is not covered, let her also be shorn. But if it is ______ for a woman to be shorn or shaved, let her be covered.” (11:6, WEB) Easy
Answer: shameful. The cited words are “… is not covered, let her also be shorn. But if it is shameful for a woman to be shorn or shaved, let her be covered.”
Q3. Multiple choice: Why does the instruction about the Lord's Supper include criticism of divisions and hunger? (11:17-34) Hard
A) The meal is unrelated to how participants treat one another. B) The chapter commends the divisions as its ideal. C) Their treatment of one another contradicts the meal they claim to share. D) Shaming the poor is described as proper worship.
Answer: C. Their treatment of one another contradicts the meal they claim to share. Paul addresses a communal practice in which some are shamed while others indulge. Christian views of Christ's presence differ, but the passage explicitly connects the meal with accountable care for the body.
Q4. Multiple choice: Which word completes “For this cause the woman ought to have authority on her ______, because of the angels.”? (11:10, WEB) Easy
A) head B) hands C) teeth D) blood
Answer: A. head. 1 Corinthians 11:10 uses head: “For this cause the woman ought to have authority on her head, because of the angels.”
Q5. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “… the Lord Jesus on the night in which he was betrayed took ______.” (11:23); “… in a manner unworthy of the Lord will be guilty of the ______ and the blood of the Lord.” (11:27, WEB) Hard
A) bread; hungry B) flour; body C) body; bread D) bread; body
Answer: D. bread; body. Verse 23 reads “… the Lord Jesus on the night in which he was betrayed took bread.”; verse 27 reads “… in a manner unworthy of the Lord will be guilty of the body and the blood of the Lord.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q6. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… I would have you know that the head of every man is ______, and the head of the woman is the man, and the head of …” (11:3, WEB) Easy
Answer: Christ. The verse reads, “… I would have you know that the head of every man is Christ, and the head of the woman is the man, and the head of …”

Answer: False. The actual wording is “… you assemble yourselves together, it is not possible to eat the Lord's supper.” It says supper, not praying.

1 Corinthians 12

Q3. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “For in one Spirit we were all baptized into one ______, whether ______ or Greeks, whether bond or free; and were all given to drink into …” (12:13, WEB) Medium
Answer: body; Jews. The wording is “For in one Spirit we were all baptized into one body, whether Jews or Greeks, whether bond or free; and were all given to drink into …” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q4. Multiple choice: How does Paul's question about whether all speak in tongues relate to his body image? (12:12-30) Hard
A) The one body contains different gifts and members rather than requiring every member to perform the same function. B) A less visible member is not needed. C) Diversity proves that there can be no unity. D) Every member must possess every identical gift.
Answer: A. The one body contains different gifts and members rather than requiring every member to perform the same function. The course treats tongues as a gift rather than the necessary sign that every believer has the Spirit. This passage explicitly emphasizes diversity within one Spirit-given body.
Q5. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “If the whole body were an hands, where would the hearing be? If the whole were hearing, where would the smelling be?” (12:17, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “If the whole body were an eye, where would the hearing be? If the whole were hearing, where would the smelling be?” It says eye, not hands.

Answer: A. foot. The passage specifies foot: “If the foot would say, "Because I'm not the hand, I'm not part of the body," it is not therefore not part of the body.”
Q7. Q3. Multiple choice: What does love contribute to Paul's surrounding discussion of spiritual gifts? (13:1-13) Hard

Answer: A. Without love, impressive gifts and sacrifices fail their proper purpose. The chapter belongs within the discussion of the church's life, not only romantic relationships. The course identifies love as the motive and practice of Christian devotion.

Q4. Multiple choice: What must be considered when interpreting the instructions about women's speech? (14:26-40; 11:5) Hard

Answer: A. The aim of orderly edification and the letter's earlier reference to women praying and prophesying. Christians differ over the scope and setting of the restriction. The text should be read with the whole discussion rather than used to pretend there is no interpretive question about how the passages fit together.

1 Corinthians 15
Q1. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… "All things are put in subjection," it is evident that he is ______ who subjected all things to him.”? (15:27, WEB) Medium
A) received B) appeared C) hundred D) excepted
Answer: D. excepted. The passage specifies excepted: “… "All things are put in subjection," it is evident that he is excepted who subjected all things to him.”
Q2. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “But by the grace of God I am what I am. His ______ which was bestowed on me was not futile, but I worked more than …” (15:10, WEB) Easy
Answer: grace. The cited words are “But by the grace of God I am what I am. His grace which was bestowed on me was not futile, but I worked more than …”
Q3. Multiple choice: Why is bodily resurrection central to Paul's argument? (15:12-28, 35-49) Hard
A) Future hope is described as wholly unrelated to Christ. B) Christ's resurrection is joined to believers' future hope and transformed embodied life. C) The chapter defines the body as a mistake God will never redeem. D) Paul says Christ's resurrection has no bearing on believers.
Answer: B. Christ's resurrection is joined to believers' future hope and transformed embodied life. Paul does not treat salvation as simple escape from an evil physical creation. The course likewise rejects the idea that the body itself is inherently bad.
Q4. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “For since ______ came by man, the ______ of the dead also came by man.” (15:21, WEB) Medium
Answer: death; resurrection. The wording is “For since death came by man, the resurrection of the dead also came by man.” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q5. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… witnesses of God, because we testified about God that he raised up ______, whom he didn't raise up, if it is so that the dead are …”? (15:15, WEB) Easy
A) Hosea B) Gallio C) Isaac D) Christ
Answer: D. Christ. 1 Corinthians 15:15 uses Christ: “… witnesses of God, because we testified about God that he raised up Christ, whom he didn't raise up, if it is so that the dead are …”
Q6. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “If I fought with animals at Damascus for human purposes, what does it profit me? If the dead are not raised, then "let us eat and …” (15:32, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “If I fought with animals at Ephesus for human purposes, what does it profit me? If the dead are not raised, then "let us eat and …” It says Ephesus, not Damascus.
Q7. Q1. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… to be clothed, that what is mortal may be swallowed up by ______.” (5:4, WEB) Easy

Q2. Multiple choice: How does reconciliation shape the community's mission? (5:14-21) Hard
A) Paul says Christ's love has no effect on how people live. B) Restored relationship removes every responsibility toward others. C) God reconciles through Christ and entrusts the message of reconciliation to His ambassadors. D) The ambassadors produce reconciliation without God.
Answer: C. God reconciles through Christ and entrusts the message of reconciliation to His ambassadors. The course describes salvation as restored relationship that gives believers a responsibility toward others. The initiative is God's; the ambassadors do not present themselves as independent saviors.
Q3. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… of our tent is dissolved, we have a building from God, a ______ not made with hands, eternal, in the heavens.” (5:1, WEB) Easy
